I have enough information to give a complete analysis. Here are the findings:

---

**Efficiency findings for Phase 45 diff**

**Finding 1 — journal_helpers.rs ~line 210: redundant second inode lookup for `file_name`**

`self.inodes.get(ino)` is called a second time just to read `i.name` (~line 210), but `name` is also in `InodeKind::File` (or the inode root) that was already extracted in the first `self.inodes.get(ino)` block (~line 155). The two lookups hit the same `DashMap` bucket. The fix is to pull `name` out in the first destructure. Cost: one extra hash-map lookup + `String::clone()` per write.

**Finding 2 — journal_helpers.rs ~lines 235–244: three more separate inode lookups after the first**

After the large destructure of `ino`, the helper makes three more independent calls to `self.inodes.get(ino)` — for `write_gen`, for `parent_ino`, and to extract `file_name` (above). All four values live in `InodeData` which is fetched once each time. Consolidate into a single `self.inodes.get(ino)` that grabs `name`, `write_generation`, and `parent_ino` alongside the `InodeKind::File` fields. Cost: three extra map lookups per write op.

**Finding 3 — journal_helpers.rs ~lines 287, 318, 319, 334, 335, 341: `encrypted_file_key_hex` and `iv_hex` cloned three times each into `FileMetadata`, `JournalOp::UploadFile`, and `UploadJournalResult`**

`encrypted_file_key_hex` is cloned at lines 287 (`wrapped_key_hex`), 334 (`file_key_encrypted`), and again moved into `UploadJournalResult`. `iv_hex` is cloned at lines 318 and 335. These are hex strings of fixed small sizes (≤128 chars), so this is not catastrophic, but three allocations for two strings that could be produced once and shared is avoidable. The caller-result struct (`UploadJournalResult`) already owns the final copy. The `JournalOp` and `FileMetadata` could share references if both were built before moving, or `wrapped_key_hex` could be eliminated (it equals `encrypted_file_key_hex`).

Specifically, `let wrapped_key_hex = encrypted_file_key_hex.clone();` (line 287) is a pure no-op alias that exists only because the variable was renamed — this clone is entirely pointless. `wrapped_key_hex` is used once in `JournalOp::UploadFile { wrapped_key_hex }` and then `encrypted_file_key_hex` is cloned again for `FileMetadata::file_key_encrypted`. The two could be unified.

**Finding 4 — journal_helpers.rs ~line 232 / ~line 341: `versions_for_meta` cloned once into `FileMetadata`, then moved into `UploadJournalResult`**

`versions_for_meta = new_versions.as_ref().filter(...).cloned()` clones the `Vec<VersionEntry>` once. Then `FileMetadata { versions: versions_for_meta.clone(), .. }` clones it again before `versions_for_meta` is moved into the result struct (line 355). Since `FileMetadata` is built before the move, this second clone is necessary for current ownership — but if `FileMetadata` and `UploadJournalResult` were built in the opposite order (build the struct last, move `versions_for_meta` into it, and clone just for `FileMetadata`), only one `Vec` allocation would exist at a time. Minor but the versions `Vec` can hold many `VersionEntry` structs.

**Finding 5 — lib.rs `resolve_folder_key_cached` ~line 1440: `.clone()` on cache hit clones the entire key `Vec<u8>` (32 bytes)**

`return Ok(key.clone())` on a cache hit. Returning an owned copy on every hit is correct for ownership, but the 32-byte key is small. This is not a real problem — just confirming the memoization is sound. The BFS (`resolve_folder_key`) is correctly avoided on hit. No issue here.

**Finding 6 — lib.rs `replay_for_vault` ~line 1206: `root_folder_key.to_vec()` allocates a fresh `Vec` to seed the cache even though `root_folder_key: &[u8]` is already available for every BFS call**

`folder_key_cache.insert(root_ipns_name.to_string(), root_folder_key.to_vec())` allocates a `Vec<u8>` copy of the root key upfront so the cache can serve it without BFS. This is intentional (the comment confirms it) and correct — `root_folder_key` is borrowed and the map needs owned values. This is not wasteful.

**Summary of genuine findings (non-trivial):**

| File | Approx line | Issue | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|
| `journal_helpers.rs` | ~210 | Redundant `inodes.get(ino)` for `file_name` after first destructure already has inode in scope | 1 extra map lookup + `String::clone()` per write |
| `journal_helpers.rs` | ~235–244 | Three separate `inodes.get(ino)` for `write_gen`, `parent_ino`, `file_name` — should be one | 3 extra map lookups per write |
| `journal_helpers.rs` | ~287 | `let wrapped_key_hex = encrypted_file_key_hex.clone()` is a pointless rename-clone; `wrapped_key_hex` and `encrypted_file_key_hex` are the same string | 1 unnecessary `String` allocation per write |
| `journal_helpers.rs` | ~341 | `versions_for_meta.clone()` for `FileMetadata` then moved into result — build order forces double-clone of potentially multi-entry `Vec<VersionEntry>` | 1 unnecessary `Vec<VersionEntry>` clone |

The memoization in `resolve_folder_key_cached` is correct — BFS is avoided on hits, and only `Vec<u8>` key bytes (32 bytes) are cloned on each hit, not any large structure. The `deser_opt_string` in `queue.rs` is clean (deserializes into `Option<String>` once, no extra allocation). The rest of the diff is whitespace/formatting only.

Now I have a clear picture. Here are the findings:

---

**Simplification findings**

**1. `crates/fuse/src/journal_helpers.rs`, line 287 — dead alias `wrapped_key_hex`**

`let wrapped_key_hex = encrypted_file_key_hex.clone();` is created immediately after `encrypted_file_key_hex` and used exactly once in the `JournalOp::UploadFile` struct literal on line 317. Both variables hold the same value; the alias adds a reader-visible name collision with the struct field and an extra clone. Drop the alias and write `wrapped_key_hex: encrypted_file_key_hex.clone()` inline.

**2. `crates/fuse/src/journal_helpers.rs`, lines 207–244 — four separate `self.inodes.get(ino)` lookups for `file_name`, `write_gen`, and `parent_ino`**

After the big destructuring at lines 153–201 already called `self.inodes.get(ino)` and extracted everything from `InodeKind::File`, the function hits `self.inodes.get(ino)` three more times just to get `name`, `write_generation`, and `parent_ino`. These three fields are available on `InodeData` (not inside `kind`) and should have been pulled from the same borrow. A single `if let Some(inode) = self.inodes.get(ino)` block early in the function can extract `name`, `write_generation`, `parent_ino`, and all `kind` fields in one pass, eliminating three redundant hashtable lookups. The `_ => default` fallback arm in the tuple-destructure can cover the `None`/non-File case as it does today.

**3. `crates/fuse/src/journal_helpers.rs`, lines 290–310 — `file_meta_ipns_name.as_ref().and_then(|_|)` gate on the ipns-key wrap is opaque**

The outer `.and_then(|_|)` discards the `Some` value and exists solely to skip the block when `file_meta_ipns_name` is `None`. The inner `file_ipns_private_key.as_ref().map(...).or_else(...)` then unconditionally proceeds. Reading it requires tracing two nested `Option` chains to understand what gates on what. The simpler form: `let file_ipns_key_hex = if file_meta_ipns_name.is_none() { None } else { ... }` (or a guard `file_meta_ipns_name.as_ref()?;` via `then_some`/`and`) removes the disposable capture and makes the intent obvious.

**4. `crates/fuse/src/journal_helpers.rs`, line 413 vs. `build_upload_journal_entry` line 214 — duplicated `SystemTime::now().duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)...as_millis() as u64` pattern**

Both builders compute `now_ms` identically inline. A private free function `now_ms() -> u64` (three lines) would remove the copy-paste across the two builders and from wherever the same pattern recurs in `lib.rs`. Low cost, and it's the natural partner to the module's stated purpose as a shared builder.

**5. `crates/fuse/src/journal_helpers.rs`, `MkdirJournalResult` line 90 — `parent_metadata` field shipped but never used by either platform caller**

`MkdirJournalResult.parent_metadata` (type `FolderMetadata`) is destructured on the Windows path (line 2645 in the diff) with `..` — i.e., it is explicitly not used. On the macOS/Linux path (write_ops.rs) the same field is ignored. It is returned from `build_folder_metadata` as the first element of the 5-tuple, but neither caller needs it; only `parent_folder_key`, `parent_ipns_key`, `parent_ipns_name`, and `parent_old_cid` are used in the spawn. Dropping the field saves the allocation and tightens the struct contract. If it needs to stay for a future caller, a `#[allow(dead_code)]` should at least annotate it.

**6. `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs`, lines 1694–1700 — `if parent_ipns_key_hex.is_empty() { return Err(...) } else { decode... }` form repeated identically in both `replay_mkdir_entry` and `replay_upload_entry`**

Both functions open with the same guard pattern (return early on empty hex, otherwise decode + ECIES-unwrap). A private `unwrap_journaled_ipns_key(hex: &str, label: &str, private_key: &[u8]) -> Result<Vec<u8>, String>` helper would deduplicate the decode/unwrap/error-message chain (5–8 lines each). The `label` argument differentiates the error string for `MkdirPublish` vs. `UploadFile`.

**7. `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs`, `deser_opt_string` — correct, but the body can be a one-liner**

```rust
fn deser_opt_string<'de, D: serde::Deserializer<'de>>(d: D) -> Result<Option<String>, D::Error> {
    let s: Option<String> = Option::deserialize(d)?;
    Ok(s.filter(|v| !v.is_empty()))
}
```

This is already concise; not a real finding. Clean as-is.

**Summary of actionable items ranked by bang-for-buck:**

| # | File | Line(s) | Issue |
|---|------|---------|-------|
| 1 | `journal_helpers.rs` | 287 | Dead `wrapped_key_hex` alias — remove |
| 2 | `journal_helpers.rs` | 207–244 | 3× redundant `inodes.get(ino)` — collapse into initial borrow |
| 3 | `journal_helpers.rs` | 90 | `parent_metadata` in `MkdirJournalResult` unused by both callers — drop or annotate |
| 4 | `journal_helpers.rs` | 290–310 | Opaque `and_then(\|_\|)` guard — replace with `is_none()` early-return or `then` |
| 5 | `lib.rs` | ~1696, ~1732 | Identical ipns-key guard/decode duplicated in two replay fns — extract helper |
| 6 | `journal_helpers.rs` | 214, 413 | `now_ms` computation duplicated — extract private fn |

I now have sufficient context for a complete altitude review. Here are my findings:

---

**Altitude Review — Phase 45 Desktop FUSE Write-Journal/Replay Cleanup**

---

**Finding 1 — `default_journal_dir` + `JOURNAL_MAX_RETRIES` in the wrong crate**

- **File:** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs`, lines ~38–56
- **Summary:** `default_journal_dir()` and `JOURNAL_MAX_RETRIES` live in the Tauri desktop app, but the `WriteQueue` type they configure lives in `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs`.
- **Cost:** `WriteQueue::new` takes `(journal_dir, max_retries)` as parameters, but the canonical values for both parameters are defined one layer above the type that owns them. Any future consumer of `WriteQueue` outside the desktop app (a CLI tool, a test binary, a daemon crate) must either re-derive the same path/constant or take a dependency on the Tauri app crate. The retry constant is the more acute problem: `WriteQueue` itself decides what "Failed" means (it checks `retries >= max_retries` internally), so the semantics of `5` are meaningful only in the context of `WriteQueue`'s behavior, yet the value is defined outside it. If the SDK changes the retry semantics, the constant stays silently stale in the app.
- **Where it should live:** `default_journal_dir()` should be a method or associated function on `WriteQueue` (or a module-level fn in `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs`), taking no arguments. `JOURNAL_MAX_RETRIES` should be a `pub const` in `crates/sdk` alongside `WriteQueue`. The desktop app and daemon call sites collapse to `WriteQueue::default_journal_dir()` and `WriteQueue::DEFAULT_MAX_RETRIES`.

---

**Finding 2 — `IpnsResolveOutcome` defined in `crates/fuse/src/error.rs` but is not a FUSE error**

- **File:** `crates/fuse/src/error.rs`, lines ~1–18
- **Summary:** `IpnsResolveOutcome` is an outcome classifier for the replay path's IPNS resolution, placed in the FUSE crate's error module alongside `FuseError`.
- **Cost:** `IpnsResolveOutcome` is a protocol-layer concern (IPNS resolve result classification), not a FUSE filesystem concern. It is used exclusively by `resolve_ipns_for_replay` in `lib.rs`, which is itself `#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]`. The type has no relationship to `FuseError` — it does not implement `thiserror::Error`. Placing it in `error.rs` teaches future engineers to put IPNS protocol outcomes there, which will accumulate. The concrete pain point is that the WinFsp replay path needs to `use crate::error::IpnsResolveOutcome` from a FUSE-labeled module, creating a conceptual mismatch that will confuse the next person distinguishing "things that go wrong in FUSE callbacks" from "IPNS resolution states".
- **Where it should live:** Either at the top of `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs` (already the home of `resolve_ipns_for_replay` and the replay machinery) or in a dedicated `crates/fuse/src/ipns_types.rs` alongside related IPNS utilities. It should not be in `error.rs`.

---

**Finding 3 — `deser_opt_string` compat shim is placed at the right layer but its scope is too narrow**

- **File:** `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs`, lines ~4991–5001
- **Summary:** `deser_opt_string` is a `fn` (not `pub`) inside `queue.rs`; it is used by exactly one field on one enum variant. This is correct — the shim lives at the type boundary (the `JournalOp` enum serialization), not spread across call sites.
- **Verdict:** Placement is correct. The `#[serde(default, deserialize_with = "deser_opt_string")]` attribute encapsulates the compat concern at the type definition. No finding.

---

**Finding 4 — `resolve_ipns_for_replay` is a module-private `async fn` in `lib.rs` wrapping `PublishCoordinator::resolve_sequence`; the substring match moves but the abstraction boundary is still the replay closure**

- **File:** `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs`, lines ~703–717
- **Summary:** The `resolve_ipns_for_replay` helper centralizes the `.to_lowercase().contains("not found")` check into one place and returns a typed `IpnsResolveOutcome`. The abstraction is correct: the string matching lives once, callers match on the enum.
- **Observation:** `spawn_bin_entry_publish` (also in `lib.rs`) still has a raw `.to_lowercase().contains("not found")` inline check (diff lines ~971–975) — it was not unified to use `resolve_ipns_for_replay`. This is a residual inconsistency: the type exists to replace exactly this pattern, but one existing call site was left behind. This is a mild altitude issue — the new abstraction is incomplete, and a future regression could revert to string matching elsewhere without noticing the pattern already has a home.
- **Where it should be fixed:** `spawn_bin_entry_publish`'s bin IPNS not-found check should call `resolve_ipns_for_replay` and match on `IpnsResolveOutcome::NotFound`, the same as the replay path now does.

---

**Finding 5 — `UploadJournalResult.plaintext` leaks plaintext through the result struct**

- **File:** `crates/fuse/src/journal_helpers.rs`, line ~198
- **Summary (altitude, not security):** The `UploadJournalResult` struct carries a `pub plaintext: Vec<u8>` field. The module-level doc comment explicitly says "ciphertext only; plaintext is never stored in the result struct", which is contradicted by the field's existence.
- **Cost from an altitude standpoint:** The builder is supposed to abstract the encrypt→journal-entry construction; exposing `plaintext` through the result means the caller still handles the plaintext lifecycle (inserting into `pending_content`). This is a second responsibility leaking out of the builder. The `pending_content.insert(ino, result.plaintext.clone())` call at the WinFsp call site is the exact operation that could be owned by the builder or at least modeled without the builder carrying the raw bytes back. This isn't a correctness bug (the bytes are not persisted to disk), but it is an abstraction leak: callers of the builder must now handle plaintext, which defeats the claimed security invariant in the doc comment.
- **Recommendation:** Either rename the field to make it clear it is transient in-memory data (e.g., `pending_content_bytes`) and correct the doc comment, or move the `pending_content.insert` step into the builder's post-commit callback so the struct never needs to surface plaintext at all.

---

**Summary**

| # | File | Altitude Issue |
|---|------|---------------|
| 1 | `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs` | `default_journal_dir` + `JOURNAL_MAX_RETRIES` belong in `crates/sdk`, not the app crate — constants that govern `WriteQueue` semantics should live with `WriteQueue` |
| 2 | `crates/fuse/src/error.rs` | `IpnsResolveOutcome` is an IPNS protocol type in a FUSE error module; should live in `lib.rs` or `ipns_types.rs` |
| 3 | `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs` (spawn_bin_entry_publish) | Residual inline `.contains("not found")` left behind while `resolve_ipns_for_replay` was introduced to centralize exactly this; one existing call site missed |
| 4 | `crates/fuse/src/journal_helpers.rs` | `UploadJournalResult.plaintext` contradicts the struct's own doc comment and leaks plaintext lifecycle responsibility back to callers |

Here are the reuse findings for this phase:

---

**Finding 1 — `now_ms` inline chain duplicated inside the new module**

- File: `crates/fuse/src/journal_helpers.rs`, lines 214–217 and 413–416
- Summary: The 4-line `SystemTime::now().duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH).as_millis() as u64` chain is copy-pasted twice within the same new file (`build_upload_journal_entry` and `build_mkdir_journal_entry`).
- Cost: `crates/sdk/src/registry.rs` has a private `now_ms() -> u64` doing exactly this (line 233). That function is private to `sdk`, so it isn't directly callable — but the correct fix is to add a `pub(crate) fn now_ms() -> u64` to `crates/fuse/src/helpers.rs` (which is already the home for shared FUSE utilities) and call it from both builders. Instead, the phase introduced a second instance of copy-paste within its own new module.

---

**Finding 2 — `parent_ipns_key_hex_for_journal` wrap-to-hex pattern asymmetric between upload and mkdir builders**

- File: `crates/fuse/src/journal_helpers.rs`, lines 261–283 (upload) vs 419–428 (mkdir)
- Summary: Both builders compute `parent_ipns_key_hex_for_journal` via `wrap_key → hex::encode → fallback to empty string on error`, but use different idioms: the upload variant uses `.map_err(log).ok().unwrap_or_default()` while the mkdir variant uses `.unwrap_or_else(|e| { log; String::new() })`. The intent is identical but the code is not shared.
- Cost: This is the exact kind of sub-helper the phase's own consolidation goal targeted. A private `fn wrap_key_to_hex(key: &[u8], pub_key: &[u8], label: &str) -> String` in `journal_helpers.rs` (or `helpers.rs`) would unify all three occurrences (upload parent, mkdir parent, mkdir child at line 431) and also remove the style divergence. No existing symbol to call — this helper needs to be extracted from the new code.

---

**Finding 3 — `hex::encode(cipherbox_crypto::utils::generate_random_bytes(16))` duplicated for entry ID**

- File: `crates/fuse/src/journal_helpers.rs`, lines 313 and 443
- Summary: Both `build_upload_journal_entry` and `build_mkdir_journal_entry` generate the journal entry `id` with the same one-liner. No helper exists anywhere for this pattern.
- Cost: Trivially extractable as a private `fn generate_entry_id() -> String` in `journal_helpers.rs`. The `JournalEntry::id` field docs in `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs` describe it as "hex-encoded 16 random bytes" — that invariant is now expressed in two places that can silently diverge (e.g. if the length changes to 32).

---

**Finding 4 — `spawn_bin_entry_publish` stringly-typed "not found" check not converted**

- File: `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs`, ~line 573 (the `if e_str.to_lowercase().contains("not found")` branch inside `spawn_bin_entry_publish`)
- Summary: The phase introduced `IpnsResolveOutcome` and `resolve_ipns_for_replay` specifically to eliminate brittle substring matches. The comment on `resolve_ipns_for_replay` (line 209) says "centralises the brittle substring match (#19)". But `spawn_bin_entry_publish` retains the same `.to_lowercase().contains("not found")` pattern and was only reformatted, not converted.
- Cost: `spawn_bin_entry_publish` calls `cipherbox_api_client::ipns::resolve_ipns` directly (not via `coordinator.resolve_sequence`), so `resolve_ipns_for_replay` doesn't fit verbatim. However, `IpnsResolveOutcome` itself is reusable — the bin resolve arm could call `resolve_ipns_for_replay` via a thin wrapper or inline the same three-arm match. As-is, the "not found" classification logic now has two implementations: the typed one in `resolve_ipns_for_replay` and the stringly-typed one that survived in `spawn_bin_entry_publish`. The consolidation is incomplete.

---

**Not flagged (clean):**

- `deser_opt_string` in `queue.rs`: correctly scoped private to the one module that reads on-disk JSON with the legacy field format. No other deserialization site needs this compat shim.
- `default_journal_dir` / `JOURNAL_MAX_RETRIES` in `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs`: clean deduplication of the previously repeated path-building chain.
- `resolve_folder_key_cached`: correct memoizing wrapper around the existing BFS — no pre-existing helper missed.
- `IpnsResolveOutcome` / `resolve_ipns_for_replay`: well-placed in `error.rs` / `lib.rs`; no existing typed outcome enum missed.
- `publish_file_metadata` duplication between `operations.rs` and `platform/windows/operations.rs`: pre-existing before this phase; the phase correctly aliased both via `use` for replay but did not introduce new duplication.

1	//! Shared journal-entry builders for the CipherBox FUSE filesystem.
2	//!
3	//! This module provides `build_upload_journal_entry` and
4	//! `build_mkdir_journal_entry` as inherent methods on [`CipherBoxFS`], so both
5	//! the fuser (macOS/Linux) and WinFsp (Windows) write paths share a single
6	//! implementation of the encrypt → ECIES-wrap → resolve-parent-IPNS →
7	//! build-`JournalEntry` steps.
8	//!
9	//! Each platform keeps its own reply/return machinery, in-memory inode
10	//! mutations, and background spawn — only the entry-build steps live here.
11	//!
12	//! ## Security invariants
13	//!
14	//! - `UploadJournalResult` carries `ciphertext` only; plaintext is never
15	//!   stored in the result struct.
16	//! - Each key is ECIES-wrapped exactly once.  The caller must not re-wrap.
17	//! - `is_first_publish` is threaded through to the caller so the per-file
18	//!   IPNS sequence number is computed correctly (§Pitfall 4 in 45-RESEARCH.md).
19	
20	#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]
21	use crate::inode::{InodeKind, ROOT_INO};
22	
23	/// Result returned by [`CipherBoxFS::build_upload_journal_entry`].
24	///
25	/// Carries the built [`cipherbox_sdk::JournalEntry`] and every field the
26	/// caller's inode-mutation and spawn block needs.  The `ciphertext` field
27	/// holds the AES-256-GCM encrypted file content; plaintext is never stored.
28	#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]
29	pub struct UploadJournalResult {
30	    /// The fully-built journal entry ready for `journal.put`.
31	    pub entry: cipherbox_sdk::JournalEntry,
32	    /// AES-256-GCM encrypted file content.
33	    pub ciphertext: Vec<u8>,
34	    /// Decrypted plaintext — used to populate `pending_content` in the
35	    /// in-memory cache so reads after write return the correct bytes without a
36	    /// network round-trip.
37	    pub plaintext: Vec<u8>,
38	    /// The `FileMetadata` struct ready for the per-file IPNS publish.
39	    pub file_meta: cipherbox_core::folder::FileMetadata,
40	    /// Raw Ed25519 private key for the per-file IPNS record (if present).
41	    /// Zeroized on drop.
42	    pub file_ipns_private_key: Option<zeroize::Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>>,
43	    /// IPNS name for the per-file metadata record (if present).
44	    pub file_meta_ipns_name: Option<String>,
45	    /// Folder key used to encrypt the per-file IPNS record (if present).
46	    pub folder_key_for_file_meta: Option<Vec<u8>>,
47	    /// ECIES-hex-encoded file key, as stored in the inode and journal.
48	    pub encrypted_file_key_hex: String,
49	    /// Hex-encoded AES-GCM IV.
50	    pub iv_hex: String,
51	    /// Plaintext size in bytes (after encryption).
52	    pub file_size: u64,
53	    /// Versioning history for the new file metadata entry.
54	    pub versions_for_meta: Option<Vec<cipherbox_core::folder::VersionEntry>>,
55	    /// Inode number of the parent folder.
56	    pub parent_ino: u64,
57	    /// CID of the previous version of this file (for unpin after upload).
58	    pub old_file_cid: Option<String>,
59	    /// CIDs of versions pruned by the versioning policy (to be unpinned).
60	    pub pruned_cids: Vec<String>,
61	    /// Write generation at the time the helper was called (before any inode
62	    /// mutation by the caller).
63	    pub write_gen: u64,
64	    /// Whether this is the first publish for the per-file IPNS record.
65	    /// Passed to `publish_file_metadata` so the sequence number starts at 0.
66	    pub is_first_publish: bool,
67	}
68	
69	/// Result returned by [`CipherBoxFS::build_mkdir_journal_entry`].
70	///
71	/// Carries the built [`cipherbox_sdk::JournalEntry`] and every field the
72	/// caller's spawn block needs to upload the initial folder metadata and
73	/// publish the new child + parent IPNS records.
74	#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]
75	pub struct MkdirJournalResult {
76	    /// The fully-built journal entry ready for `journal.put`.
77	    pub entry: cipherbox_sdk::JournalEntry,
78	    /// Encrypted initial folder metadata bytes ready for IPFS upload.
79	    pub json_bytes: Vec<u8>,
80	    /// Raw Ed25519 private key for the new child folder IPNS record.
81	    /// Zeroized on drop.
82	    pub ipns_private_key: zeroize::Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>,
83	    /// TEE-ECIES-wrapped child IPNS private key (for key rotation / TEE
84	    /// republishing).  `None` if no TEE public key is configured.
85	    pub encrypted_ipns_for_tee: Option<String>,
86	    /// Current TEE key epoch (forwarded verbatim from `CipherBoxFS`).
87	    pub tee_key_epoch: Option<u32>,
88	    /// IPNS name for the newly created child folder.
89	    pub ipns_name: String,
90	    /// Parent folder metadata snapshot (with the new child already appended)
91	    /// ready for the parent IPNS publish.
92	    pub parent_metadata: cipherbox_core::folder::FolderMetadata,
93	    /// Raw parent folder encryption key.
94	    pub parent_folder_key: Vec<u8>,
95	    /// Raw Ed25519 private key for the parent folder IPNS record.
96	    pub parent_ipns_key: Vec<u8>,
97	    /// IPNS name of the parent folder.
98	    pub parent_ipns_name: String,
99	    /// CID of the parent folder's previous metadata blob (for unpin on
100	    /// successful publish).
101	    pub parent_old_cid: Option<String>,
102	    /// Inode number assigned to the new child folder.
103	    pub ino: u64,
104	}

106	#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]
107	impl crate::CipherBoxFS {
108	    /// Build a [`JournalEntry`] for an upload (file write) without writing the
109	    /// entry to disk, mutating inodes, or spawning.
110	    ///
111	    /// Performs:
112	    /// 1. Read plaintext from the temp file handle.
113	    /// 2. AES-256-GCM encrypt + ECIES-wrap the file key.
114	    /// 3. Extract previous file metadata from the inode table.
115	    /// 4. Apply versioning policy.
116	    /// 5. Resolve `parent_folder_ipns_name` and ECIES-wrap the parent IPNS key.
117	    /// 6. ECIES-wrap the per-file IPNS key (if present).
118	    /// 7. Build and return a [`JournalOp::UploadFile`] entry.
119	    ///
120	    /// # Security
121	    ///
122	    /// Keys are wrapped exactly once.  The returned `UploadJournalResult`
123	    /// carries ciphertext only; the file key is cleared before return.
124	    pub fn build_upload_journal_entry(
125	        &self,
126	        ino: u64,
127	        handle: &crate::file_handle::OpenFileHandle,
128	        is_new_file: bool,
129	    ) -> Result<UploadJournalResult, String> {
130	        let plaintext = handle.read_all()?;
131	
132	        let mut file_key = cipherbox_crypto::utils::generate_file_key();
133	        let iv = cipherbox_crypto::utils::generate_iv();
134	
135	        let ciphertext = cipherbox_crypto::aes::encrypt_aes_gcm(&plaintext, &file_key, &iv)
136	            .map_err(|e| format!("File encryption failed: {}", e))?;
137	
138	        let wrapped_key = cipherbox_crypto::ecies::wrap_key(&file_key, &self.public_key)
139	            .map_err(|e| format!("Key wrapping failed: {}", e))?;
140	
141	        // Zeroize raw file key before any fallible path can return.
142	        cipherbox_crypto::utils::clear_bytes(&mut file_key);
143	
144	        let (
145	            old_file_cid,
146	            old_encrypted_key,
147	            old_iv,
148	            old_size,
149	            old_mode,
150	            existing_versions,
151	            file_ipns_private_key,
152	            file_meta_ipns_name,
153	        ) = self
154	            .inodes
155	            .get(ino)
156	            .map(|inode| match &inode.kind {
157	                InodeKind::File {
158	                    cid,
159	                    encrypted_file_key,
160	                    iv,
161	                    size,
162	                    encryption_mode,
163	                    versions,
164	                    file_ipns_private_key,
165	                    file_meta_ipns_name,
166	                    ..
167	                } => (
168	                    if cid.is_empty() {
169	                        None
170	                    } else {
171	                        Some(cid.clone())
172	                    },
173	                    encrypted_file_key.clone(),
174	                    iv.clone(),
175	                    *size,
176	                    encryption_mode.clone(),
177	                    versions.clone(),
178	                    file_ipns_private_key.clone(),
179	                    file_meta_ipns_name.clone(),
180	                ),
181	                _ => (
182	                    None,
183	                    String::new(),
184	                    String::new(),
185	                    0,
186	                    "GCM".to_string(),
187	                    None,
188	                    None,
189	                    None,
190	                ),
191	            })
192	            .unwrap_or((
193	                None,
194	                String::new(),
195	                String::new(),
196	                0,
197	                "GCM".to_string(),
198	                None,
199	                None,
200	                None,
201	            ));
202	
203	        let encrypted_file_key_hex = hex::encode(&wrapped_key);
204	        let iv_hex = hex::encode(&iv);
205	        let file_size = plaintext.len() as u64;
206	
207	        let file_name = self
208	            .inodes
209	            .get(ino)
210	            .map(|i| i.name.clone())
211	            .unwrap_or_default();
212	        let mime_type = crate::helpers::mime_from_extension(&file_name);
213	
214	        let now_ms = std::time::SystemTime::now()
215	            .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)
216	            .unwrap_or_default()
217	            .as_millis() as u64;
218	
219	        let (new_versions, pruned_cids) = crate::helpers::apply_versioning(
220	            existing_versions,
221	            &old_file_cid,
222	            &old_encrypted_key,
223	            &old_iv,
224	            old_size,
225	            &old_mode,
226	            now_ms,
227	            self.max_versions_per_file,
228	            self.version_cooldown_ms,
229	            ino,
230	        );
231	
232	        let versions_for_meta = new_versions.as_ref().filter(|v| !v.is_empty()).cloned();
233	
234	        let write_gen = self
235	            .inodes
236	            .get(ino)
237	            .map(|i| i.write_generation)
238	            .unwrap_or(0);
239	
240	        let parent_ino = self
241	            .inodes
242	            .get(ino)
243	            .map(|i| i.parent_ino)
244	            .unwrap_or(ROOT_INO);
245	
246	        let folder_key_for_file_meta = self.get_folder_key(parent_ino);
247	
248	        // Resolve parent IPNS name for stable journal entry (D-02).
249	        let parent_folder_ipns_name = self
250	            .inodes
251	            .get(parent_ino)
252	            .and_then(|inode| match &inode.kind {
253	                InodeKind::Root { ipns_name, .. } => ipns_name.clone(),
254	                InodeKind::Folder { ipns_name, .. } => Some(ipns_name.clone()),
255	                _ => None,
256	            })
257	            .unwrap_or_else(|| self.root_ipns_name.clone());
258	
259	        // CR-01: journal the user-ECIES-wrapped parent IPNS key so replay can
260	        // sign and publish the parent IPNS record at crash-recovery time.
261	        let parent_ipns_key_hex_for_journal = self
262	            .inodes
263	            .get(parent_ino)
264	            .and_then(|inode| match &inode.kind {
265	                InodeKind::Root {
266	                    ipns_private_key, ..
267	                } => ipns_private_key.as_deref(),
268	                InodeKind::Folder {
269	                    ipns_private_key, ..
270	                } => ipns_private_key.as_deref(),
271	                _ => None,
272	            })
273	            .and_then(|raw_key| {
274	                cipherbox_crypto::wrap_key(raw_key, &self.public_key)
275	                    .map(|w| hex::encode(&w))
276	                    .map_err(|e| {
277	                        log::warn!("Failed to wrap parent IPNS key for journal: {}", e);
278	                        e
279	                    })
280	                    .ok()
281	            })
282	            .unwrap_or_default();
283	
284	        // Build journal entry referencing ciphertext only — no plaintext (D-05).
285	        use base64::Engine;
286	        let ciphertext_b64 = base64::engine::general_purpose::STANDARD.encode(&ciphertext);
287	        let wrapped_key_hex = encrypted_file_key_hex.clone();
288	
289	        // ECIES-wrap the per-file IPNS key exactly once (CR-01, no double-wrap).
290	        let file_ipns_key_hex = file_meta_ipns_name.as_ref().and_then(|_| {
291	            file_ipns_private_key
292	                .as_ref()
293	                .map(|k| {
294	                    cipherbox_crypto::ecies::wrap_key(k, &self.public_key)
295	                        .map(|w| hex::encode(&w))
296	                        .unwrap_or_else(|e| {
297	                            log::warn!("Failed to wrap file IPNS key for journal: {}", e);
298	                            String::new()
299	                        })
300	                })
301	                .or_else(|| {
302	                    self.inodes.get(ino).and_then(|i| match &i.kind {
303	                        InodeKind::File {
304	                            file_ipns_key_encrypted_hex,
305	                            ..
306	                        } => file_ipns_key_encrypted_hex.clone(),
307	                        _ => None,
308	                    })
309	                })
310	        });
311	
312	        let entry = cipherbox_sdk::JournalEntry {
313	            id: hex::encode(cipherbox_crypto::utils::generate_random_bytes(16)),
314	            vault_root_ipns: self.root_ipns_name.clone(),
315	            op: cipherbox_sdk::JournalOp::UploadFile {
316	                ciphertext_b64,
317	                wrapped_key_hex,
318	                iv_hex: iv_hex.clone(),
319	                file_meta_ipns_name: file_meta_ipns_name.clone(),
320	                file_ipns_key_hex,
321	                parent_folder_ipns_name,
322	                parent_ipns_key_hex: parent_ipns_key_hex_for_journal,
323	                filename: file_name,
324	                size: file_size,
325	                created_at_ms: now_ms,
326	            },
327	            retries: 0,
328	            status: cipherbox_sdk::JournalEntryStatus::Pending,
329	        };
330	
331	        let file_meta = cipherbox_core::folder::FileMetadata {
332	            version: "v1".to_string(),
333	            cid: String::new(),
334	            file_key_encrypted: encrypted_file_key_hex.clone(),
335	            file_iv: iv_hex.clone(),
336	            size: file_size,
337	            mime_type,
338	            encryption_mode: "GCM".to_string(),
339	            created_at: now_ms,
340	            modified_at: now_ms,
341	            versions: versions_for_meta.clone(),
342	        };
343	
344	        Ok(UploadJournalResult {
345	            entry,
346	            ciphertext,
347	            plaintext,
348	            file_meta,
349	            file_ipns_private_key,
350	            file_meta_ipns_name,
351	            folder_key_for_file_meta,
352	            encrypted_file_key_hex,
353	            iv_hex,
354	            file_size,
355	            versions_for_meta,
356	            parent_ino,
357	            old_file_cid,
358	            pruned_cids,
359	            write_gen,
360	            is_first_publish: is_new_file,
361	        })
362	    }
363	
364	    /// Build a [`JournalEntry`] for a directory creation (mkdir) without
365	    /// writing the entry to disk, mutating inodes, or spawning.
366	    ///
367	    /// The caller is expected to have already:
368	    /// - Allocated `ino` via `fs.inodes.allocate_ino()`
369	    /// - Inserted the new child inode
370	    /// - Updated the parent inode's children + timestamps
371	    ///
372	    /// Performs:
373	    /// 1. Generate the initial encrypted folder metadata bytes.
374	    /// 2. Optionally ECIES-wrap the child IPNS key for the TEE.
375	    /// 3. Build the parent folder metadata snapshot via `build_folder_metadata`.
376	    /// 4. ECIES-wrap parent + child IPNS private keys (user key).
377	    /// 5. Build and return a [`JournalOp::MkdirPublish`] entry.
378	    ///
379	    /// # Security
380	    ///
381	    /// Each key is wrapped exactly once.  The TEE-wrapped key is stored
382	    /// separately from the user-ECIES-wrapped key in the result struct.
383	    pub fn build_mkdir_journal_entry(
384	        &self,
385	        parent_ino: u64,
386	        child_ino: u64,
387	        name: &str,
388	        folder_key: &[u8],
389	        ipns_name: &str,
390	        ipns_private_key: zeroize::Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>,
391	        encrypted_folder_key_hex: &str,
392	    ) -> Result<MkdirJournalResult, String> {
393	        let metadata = cipherbox_core::folder::FolderMetadata {
394	            version: "v2".to_string(),
395	            children: vec![],
396	        };
397	
398	        let json_bytes = crate::encrypt_metadata_to_json(&metadata, folder_key)?;
399	
400	        // TEE-wrap the child IPNS private key for republishing (if TEE configured).
401	        let encrypted_ipns_for_tee = if let Some(ref tee_key) = self.tee_public_key {
402	            let wrapped = cipherbox_crypto::wrap_key(&ipns_private_key, tee_key)
403	                .map_err(|e| format!("TEE key wrapping failed: {}", e))?;
404	            Some(hex::encode(&wrapped))
405	        } else {
406	            None
407	        };
408	        let tee_key_epoch = self.tee_key_epoch;
409	
410	        let (parent_metadata, parent_folder_key, parent_ipns_key, parent_ipns_name, parent_old_cid) =
411	            self.build_folder_metadata(parent_ino)?;
412	
413	        let mkdir_created_at_ms = std::time::SystemTime::now()
414	            .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)
415	            .unwrap_or_default()
416	            .as_millis() as u64;
417	
418	        // CR-01: journal the user-ECIES-wrapped parent IPNS key for replay signing.
419	        let parent_ipns_key_hex_for_journal =
420	            cipherbox_crypto::wrap_key(&parent_ipns_key, &self.public_key)
421	                .map(|w| hex::encode(&w))
422	                .unwrap_or_else(|e| {
423	                    // An empty string parks the entry on replay rather than
424	                    // persisting a degraded key silently.
425	                    log::warn!("Failed to wrap parent IPNS key for journal: {}", e);
426	                    String::new()
427	                });
428	
429	        // CR-03: journal the user-ECIES-wrapped child IPNS key (not TEE-wrapped).
430	        let child_ipns_key_hex_user_wrapped =
431	            cipherbox_crypto::wrap_key(&ipns_private_key, &self.public_key)
432	                .map(|w| hex::encode(&w))
433	                .unwrap_or_else(|e| {
434	                    // CR-03: never fall back to the TEE-wrapped key here — replay
435	                    // writes this into FolderEntry.ipns_private_key_encrypted which
436	                    // must be user-ECIES-wrapped.  An empty string makes replay park
437	                    // the entry rather than brick the folder with an unusable key.
438	                    log::warn!("Failed to wrap child IPNS key for journal: {}", e);
439	                    String::new()
440	                });
441	
442	        let entry = cipherbox_sdk::JournalEntry {
443	            id: hex::encode(cipherbox_crypto::utils::generate_random_bytes(16)),
444	            vault_root_ipns: self.root_ipns_name.clone(),
445	            op: cipherbox_sdk::JournalOp::MkdirPublish {
446	                child_ipns_name: ipns_name.to_string(),
447	                child_folder_key_hex: encrypted_folder_key_hex.to_string(),
448	                child_ipns_key_hex: child_ipns_key_hex_user_wrapped,
449	                parent_folder_ipns_name: parent_ipns_name.clone(),
450	                parent_ipns_key_hex: parent_ipns_key_hex_for_journal,
451	                name: name.to_string(),
452	                created_at_ms: mkdir_created_at_ms,
453	            },
454	            retries: 0,
455	            status: cipherbox_sdk::JournalEntryStatus::Pending,
456	        };
457	
458	        Ok(MkdirJournalResult {
459	            entry,
460	            json_bytes,
461	            ipns_private_key,
462	            encrypted_ipns_for_tee,
463	            tee_key_epoch,
464	            ipns_name: ipns_name.to_string(),
465	            parent_metadata,
466	            parent_folder_key,
467	            parent_ipns_key,
468	            parent_ipns_name,
469	            parent_old_cid,
470	            ino: child_ino,
471	        })
472	    }
473	}
474	
475	#[cfg(test)]
476	mod tests {
477	    // Unit tests for journal_helpers live in crates/fuse/src/lib.rs (alongside
478	    // the existing characterization tests) and in crates/sdk/src/queue.rs
479	    // (journal round-trip tests).  This module intentionally has no runtime
480	    // network dependencies so there are no tests that require a live
481	    // CipherBoxFS instance here.
482	}
483

## /simplify summary

**Applied** — 1 commit (`c22ecbefc`), all in `journal_helpers.rs`, net −2 lines, behavior-preserving (44 fuse + 48 sdk tests green, workspace check clean):

- **Extracted 3 helpers** — `current_unix_ms()`, `generate_entry_id()`, `wrap_key_to_hex()` — removing copy-paste across the two builders, including the 4 wrap-to-hex sites that had divergent idioms (Reuse + Simplify).
- **Dropped the dead `wrapped_key_hex` rename-clone** (Simplify/Efficiency).
- **Collapsed** the duplicate `write_generation`/`parent_ino` inode lookups into one (Simplify/Efficiency).
- **Replaced** the opaque `as_ref().and_then(|_| …)` guard with an explicit `is_none()` branch (Simplify).
- **Fixed** the contradictory doc comment — the journal stores ciphertext only, but the result struct *does* carry transient in-memory plaintext for the read-after-write cache (Altitude).

**Skipped, with reasons:**

- **`parent_metadata` "unused"** — false positive; verified it *is* used by both callers (`write_ops.rs:612`, `windows:211`).
- **Convert `spawn_bin_entry_publish`'s string match to the typed enum** — requirement #19 was deliberately scoped to *replay only* (verifier confirmed); the bin path uses a different resolve fn. Out of scope.
- **Move `default_journal_dir`/`JOURNAL_MAX_RETRIES` to `crates/sdk`** — a cross-crate API move beyond the diff; current placement (both consumers in the desktop app, an app-data-dir concern) is defensible.
- **Move `IpnsResolveOutcome` out of `error.rs`** — contradicts the deliberate planned placement; marginal churn.
- **Extract a replay key-unwrap helper in `lib.rs`** — touches the sensitive crash-recovery path; deferred rather than churn gated replay code in a cleanup pass.
- Minor clone nits (e.g. `versions_for_meta`) — required for ownership / negligible.

The two skipped placement findings (`default_journal_dir` location, the bin-path string match) are legitimate future cleanups if you ever want them — just out of scope for a behavior-preserving pass on this PR.

[@coderabbitai] _⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_ | _⚡ Quick win_

**Zeroize the generated file key on every error path.**

`file_key` is cleared only after both encryption and ECIES wrapping succeed. If either call returns `Err`, the raw file key is dropped without `clear_bytes`, despite the comment saying fallible paths are covered.

+        let ciphertext = match cipherbox_crypto::aes::encrypt_aes_gcm(&plaintext, &file_key, &iv) {

[@coderabbitai] _⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_ | _⚡ Quick win_

**Classify replay IPNS resolution without cache fallback.**

`resolve_ipns_for_replay` calls `PublishCoordinator::resolve_sequence`, which returns a cached sequence on resolve failure. That can turn a transient/non-404 resolve failure into `Found(cached)` and allow replay to continue instead of retaining the journal entry through the typed `Error` branch. Use a strict resolve path here, or add a strict coordinator method, so only a real IPNS resolve success becomes `Found`.

behavior that masks transient or non-404 errors by returning cached sequences,
causing the Error branch to be skipped and allowing replay to continue
incorrectly. To fix this, either call a strict resolve method on the coordinator
that bypasses the cache fallback behavior (if one exists), or create a new
strict coordinator method that performs IPNS resolution without cache fallback.
Ensure that only actual successful IPNS resolution returns
`IpnsResolveOutcome::Found`, and that transient errors or other failures flow

[@coderabbitai] _⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_ | _🏗️ Heavy lift_

**Do not publish an empty `FilePointer` as a successful replay.**

When `file_meta_ipns_name` is `None`, Step 3 skips per-file metadata publish, but Step 4 publishes a parent `FilePointer` whose locator is `""`; `replay_for_vault` can then remove the journal. The uploaded CID has no resolvable file metadata record, and multiple legacy empty-name entries also collide under `merge_folder_children`’ IPNS-key merge semantics. Return an error/park the entry unless replay can create a real IPNS name/key for this legacy case.

    pub async fn resolve_sequence(
        &self,
        api: &cipherbox_api_client::ApiClient,
        ipns_name: &str,
    ) -> Result<u64, String> {
        match cipherbox_api_client::ipns::resolve_ipns(api, ipns_name).await {
            Ok(resp) => {
                let resolved = resp.sequence_number.parse::<u64>().unwrap_or_else(|e| {
                    log::warn!(
                        "Failed to parse IPNS sequence '{}' for {}: {}",
                        resp.sequence_number,
                        ipns_name,
                        e
                    );
                    0
                });
                let cached = self.get_cached(ipns_name).unwrap_or(0);
                let seq = std::cmp::max(resolved, cached);
                self.update_cache(ipns_name, seq);
                Ok(seq)
            }
            Err(e) => match self.get_cached(ipns_name) {
                Some(cached) => {
                    log::warn!(
                        "IPNS resolve failed for {}, using cached seq {}: {}",
                        ipns_name,
                        cached,
                        e
                    );
                    Ok(cached)
                }
                None => Err(format!(
                    "IPNS resolve failed and no cached sequence for {}: {}",
                    ipns_name, e
                )),
            },
        }
    }

file: .planning/todos/pending/2026-02-14-erc-1271-contract-wallet-authentication.md
---
created: 2026-02-14
title: Add ERC-1271 contract wallet authentication support
area: auth
files:
  - apps/api/src/auth/services/siwe.service.ts
  - apps/web/src/components/auth/WalletLoginButton.tsx
---

## Problem

Phase 12.3 SIWE implementation only verifies EOA signatures via `ecrecover` (viem's `verifyMessage`). Smart contract wallets (Safe, Argent, Sequence, etc.) use ERC-1271 `isValidSignature` for signature verification, which requires an on-chain call to the wallet contract on mainnet.

Without ERC-1271 support, users with smart contract wallets will get "Invalid SIWE signature" errors when attempting to log in or link their wallet.

## Solution

TBD — key considerations:

- Backend needs an RPC connection to Ethereum mainnet to call `isValidSignature(hash, signature)` on the wallet contract
```

{

  "content": "---\ncreated: 2026-06-15\ntitle: Use a strict (cache-bypassing) IPNS resolve in replay classification\narea: fuse\nfiles:\n  - crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:217\n---\n\n## Problem\n\n`resolve_ipns_for_replay` (crates/fuse/src/lib.rs ~line 217) classifies the replay IPNS\nresolve result via `PublishCoordinator::resolve_sequence`. That coordinator method falls\nback to a cached sequence on resolve failure: `Err(e) => match self.get_cached(name) { Some(cached) => Ok(cached), None => Err(...) }`.\n\nSo a **transient / non-404** resolve failure with a cached sequence returns `Ok(cached)`,\nwhich `resolve_ipns_for_replay` maps to `IpnsResolveOutcome::Found(cached)`. Replay then\ncontinues (publishes at `cached + 1`) instead of routing through the typed\n`IpnsResolveOutcome::Error` branch that retains the journal entry for the next mount.\nThe net effect: a network blip during replay can advance the sequence off a stale cached\nvalue rather than parking the entry.\n\nThis is **pre-existing** behavior. Phase 45 (#19) replaced the old\n`.to_lowercase().contains(\"not found\")` string match with the typed enum but deliberately\npreserved the exact classification — so #19 did not change this. It was flagged by\nCodeRabbit on PR #491 and deferred as out of scope for that no-behavior-change cleanup.\n\n## Solution\n\nTBD — add a strict resolve path so only a genuine IPNS resolve success becomes `Found`:\n\n- Add a cache-bypassing method on `PublishCoordinator` (e.g. `resolve_sequence_strict`)\n  that returns `Err` on any resolve failure regardless of cache state, and call it from\n  `resolve_ipns_for_replay`; OR\n- Have `resolve_ipns_for_replay` call `cipherbox_api_client::ipns::resolve_ipns` directly\n  and classify Found / NotFound (404) / Error itself, keeping the cache only for the\n  sequence-advance step after a confirmed success.\n- This is a behavior change to crash-recovery, so it needs its own tests\n  (transient-failure-with-cache → entry retained, real 404 → first publish) and should\n  not regress the existing replay characterization tests.\n\nSource: CodeRabbit review on PR #491 (thread on crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:222).\n"
}
```

  "content": "---\ncreated: 2026-06-15\ntitle: Park legacy empty file_meta_ipns_name replay entries instead of empty FilePointer\narea: fuse\nfiles:\n  - crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1825\n---\n\n## Problem\n\nIn `replay_upload_entry` (crates/fuse/src/lib.rs ~line 1825), when `file_meta_ipns_name`\nis `None` (a legacy pre-Phase-45 journal entry that stored the `\"\"` sentinel), Step 3\nskips the per-file metadata IPNS publish, but Step 4 still builds a parent `FolderChild::File`\n`FilePointer` whose `file_meta_ipns_name` is `\"\"` (via `unwrap_or_default()`) and id\n`replay-`. `replay_for_vault` then treats the entry as published and removes it from the\njournal. The uploaded content CID ends up with **no resolvable per-file metadata record**,\nand multiple legacy empty-name entries can collide under `merge_folder_children`'s\nIPNS-key merge semantics (all keyed on the empty name).\n\nThis is **pre-existing** behavior, explicitly preserved: the code carries the comment\n\"an empty string preserves the pre-Phase-45 behavior where files without a per-file IPNS\nname are still merged into the parent via their FilePointer entry.\" Phase 45 (#18) changed\nthe `\"\"` sentinel to `Option<String>` but kept this replay behavior identical (verified as\nno-behavior-change). Flagged by CodeRabbit on PR #491 (tagged \"Heavy lift\") and deferred as\nout of scope for that cleanup phase.\n\n## Solution\n\nTBD — for the `file_meta_ipns_name == None` legacy case, either:\n\n- Park / return an error for the entry (retain in journal) rather than publishing an empty\n  `FilePointer`, so it is never marked successfully replayed without a real metadata record; OR\n- Have replay mint a fresh per-file IPNS name + key for the legacy entry so it gets a real,\n  resolvable metadata record before building the FilePointer.\n- Needs care: decide what should happen to already-published empty-locator FilePointers\n  from past replays, and add tests for the legacy-empty-name path + the multi-entry\n  merge-collision case. This is a crash-recovery behavior change — out of any\n  no-behavior-change phase; plan it as its own bug-fix work.\n\nSource: CodeRabbit review on PR #491 (thread on crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1837).\n"
}

===== resolve_ipns_for_replay + the sequence helper above it (lib.rs ~180-225) =====

/// Entry in the debounced publish queue.
#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]
pub struct PublishQueueEntry {
    pub first_dirty: std::time::Instant,
    pub pending_uploads: usize,
}

pub fn next_file_publish_sequence(
    is_first_publish: bool,
    current_sequence: Option<u64>,
) -> Result<u64, String> {
    if is_first_publish {
        return Ok(0);
    }

    current_sequence
        .map(|seq| seq + 1)
        .ok_or_else(|| "Missing current sequence for existing file IPNS record".to_string())
}

/// Classifies an IPNS resolve result into a typed [`crate::error::IpnsResolveOutcome`].
///
/// Wraps [`PublishCoordinator::resolve_sequence`] and maps:
/// - `Ok(seq)` → `Found(seq)`
/// - `Err(e)` where `e` signals 404 / "not found" → `NotFound`
/// - `Err(e)` otherwise → `Error(e)` (non-404; entry should be retained)
///
/// Centralises the brittle substring match (#19) so `replay_upload_entry` matches
/// on the typed enum rather than calling `.to_lowercase().contains("not found")` inline.
#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]
async fn resolve_ipns_for_replay(
    coordinator: &PublishCoordinator,
    api: &cipherbox_api_client::ApiClient,
    ipns_name: &str,
) -> crate::error::IpnsResolveOutcome {
    use crate::error::IpnsResolveOutcome;
    match coordinator.resolve_sequence(api, ipns_name).await {
        Ok(seq) => IpnsResolveOutcome::Found(seq),
        Err(e) if e.to_lowercase().contains("not found") || e.contains("404") => {
            IpnsResolveOutcome::NotFound
        }
        Err(e) => IpnsResolveOutcome::Error(e),
    }
}

/// Coordinates IPNS publish operations to prevent sequence number races.
#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]
pub struct PublishCoordinator {
    seq_cache: std::sync::Mutex<HashMap<String, u64>>,
    publish_locks: std::sync::Mutex<HashMap<String, Arc<tokio::sync::Mutex<()>>>>,
```

            }

    // T-45-05: not_found_outcome_drives_first_publish
    //
    // Pins the #19 branch contract: given each IpnsResolveOutcome variant, the replay
    // sequencing decision must produce the correct (is_first_publish, new_seq) pair
    // without any network access. This test exercises the classification → sequence
    // computation directly, mirroring the branch logic inside replay_upload_entry.
    //
    // NotFound  -> is_first_publish=true,  new_seq=0  (next_file_publish_sequence(true, None))
    // Found(7)  -> is_first_publish=false, new_seq=8  (current_seq + 1)
    // Error(_)  -> Err propagated (entry retained, no sequence produced)
    #[test]
    fn not_found_outcome_drives_first_publish() {
        use super::next_file_publish_sequence;
        use crate::error::IpnsResolveOutcome;

        // NotFound -> first publish at seq 0
        let outcome_not_found = IpnsResolveOutcome::NotFound;
        let (is_first, new_seq) = match outcome_not_found {
            IpnsResolveOutcome::Found(seq) => {
                (false, next_file_publish_sequence(false, Some(seq)).unwrap())
            }
            IpnsResolveOutcome::NotFound => (true, next_file_publish_sequence(true, None).unwrap()),
            IpnsResolveOutcome::Error(e) => panic!("unexpected Error variant: {}", e),
        };
        assert!(is_first, "NotFound must set is_first_publish=true");
        assert_eq!(new_seq, 0, "NotFound must produce seq 0");

        // Found(7) -> update at seq 8
        let outcome_found = IpnsResolveOutcome::Found(7);
        let (is_first, new_seq) = match outcome_found {
            IpnsResolveOutcome::Found(seq) => {
                (false, next_file_publish_sequence(false, Some(seq)).unwrap())
            }
            IpnsResolveOutcome::NotFound => (true, next_file_publish_sequence(true, None).unwrap()),
            IpnsResolveOutcome::Error(e) => panic!("unexpected Error variant: {}", e),
        };
        assert!(!is_first, "Found must set is_first_publish=false");
        assert_eq!(new_seq, 8, "Found(7) must produce seq 8");

        // Error(_) -> propagated as Err (entry retained)
        let outcome_err = IpnsResolveOutcome::Error("transient failure".to_string());
        let result: Result<(bool, u64), String> = match outcome_err {
            IpnsResolveOutcome::Found(seq) => {
                Ok((false, next_file_publish_sequence(false, Some(seq)).unwrap()))
            }
            IpnsResolveOutcome::NotFound => {
                Ok((true, next_file_publish_sequence(true, None).unwrap()))
            }

  "new_string": "async fn resolve_ipns_for_replay(\n    coordinator: &PublishCoordinator,\n    api: &cipherbox_api_client::ApiClient,\n    ipns_name: &str,\n) -> crate::error::IpnsResolveOutcome {\n    classify_resolve_outcome(coordinator.resolve_sequence(api, ipns_name).await)\n}\n\n/// Pure classification of a `resolve_sequence` result into an [`crate::error::IpnsResolveOutcome`].\n///\n/// Split out from [`resolve_ipns_for_replay`] so the brittle \"not found\" / \"404\"\n/// substring contract (#19) is unit-testable without a live API client:\n/// - `Ok(seq)` → `Found(seq)`\n/// - `Err(e)` whose text signals 404 / \"not found\" → `NotFound`\n/// - any other `Err(e)` → `Error(e)` (entry retained on replay)\n#[cfg(any(feature = \"fuse\", feature = \"winfsp\"))]\nfn classify_resolve_outcome(result: Result<u64, String>) -> crate::error::IpnsResolveOutcome {\n    use crate::error::IpnsResolveOutcome;\n    match result {\n        Ok(seq) => IpnsResolveOutcome::Found(seq),\n        Err(e) if e.to_lowercase().contains(\"not found\") || e.contains(\"404\") => {\n            IpnsResolveOutcome::NotFound\n        }\n        Err(e) => IpnsResolveOutcome::Error(e),\n    }\n}"

  "new_string": "    #[test]\n    fn next_file_publish_sequence_rejects_missing_existing_sequence() {\n        assert!(next_file_publish_sequence(false, None).is_err());\n    }\n\n    // T-45-05b: classify_resolve_outcome pins the #19 substring contract directly\n    // (the not-found / 404 classification that drives first-publish vs retain),\n    // exercising the predicate without a live API client.\n    #[test]\n    fn classify_resolve_outcome_maps_resolve_results() {\n        use super::classify_resolve_outcome;\n        use crate::error::IpnsResolveOutcome;\n\n        // Successful resolve -> Found(seq)\n        assert!(matches!(\n            classify_resolve_outcome(Ok(7)),\n            IpnsResolveOutcome::Found(7)\n        ));\n\n        // not-found / 404 signals (case-insensitive) -> NotFound (drives first publish)\n        for msg in [\n            \"record not found\",\n            \"IPNS NOT FOUND\",\n            \"HTTP 404 Not Found\",\n            \"server returned 404\",\n        ] {\n            assert!(\n                matches!(\n                    classify_resolve_outcome(Err(msg.to_string())),\n                    IpnsResolveOutcome::NotFound\n                ),\n                \"{msg:?} must classify as NotFound\"\n            );\n        }\n\n        // transient / other errors -> Error (entry retained, NOT a first publish)\n        for msg in [\"connection timeout\", \"500 internal server error\"] {\n            assert!(\n                matches!(\n                    classify_resolve_outcome(Err(msg.to_string())),\n                    IpnsResolveOutcome::Error(_)\n                ),\n                \"{msg:?} must classify as Error\"\n            );\n        }\n    }\n"
}

> AGENT

Pushed (`c7fa8d346`). Here's the honest picture on `lib.rs` coverage.

**What I added (this commit):** extracted the #19 not-found/404 classification out of the async, network-bound `resolve_ipns_for_replay` into a pure `classify_resolve_outcome(result)` helper and unit-tested it directly — `Found` / `NotFound` (case-insensitive + `404`) / `Error`. That was the one *cheaply* coverable untested branch. Fuse suite 44 → 45, behavior unchanged.

**Why the number stays low, and it's not really fixable in this PR:** the bulk of `lib.rs`'s 444 missing lines — and the reason `read_ops.rs`/`write_ops.rs`/`journal_helpers.rs` sit at **0%** — is FUSE callbacks and the `replay_*` path. Those need a **live mount + IPFS/IPNS network** to drive; they're integration/E2E territory, not unit-testable without a mocking harness. The new pure logic that *could* be unit-tested is already covered:

| New logic | Coverage |
|-----------|----------|
| `next_file_publish_sequence` | ✅ 3 tests |
| `merge_folder_children` | ✅ T-45-08 |
| `resolve_folder_key` memoization | ✅ T-45-07 |
| serde `""`→`Option` compat | ✅ queue.rs tests |
| **resolve classification (#19)** | ✅ **now added** |

So I've squeezed out the realistic unit-level wins. Reaching the 80% patch target would require driving `replay_for_vault` / `replay_upload_entry` / `replay_mkdir_entry` and the write/mkdir callbacks — which means building a **mock `ApiClient` + in-memory inode/FS fixture** harness. That's a genuine, separate effort (and it pairs naturally with the two deferred replay todos, since those fixes need exactly that harness to test).

My recommendation: either **accept the patch-coverage miss for this PR** (it's mostly mount-only glue in a no-behavior-change refactor — reasonable to note in the Codecov thread or relax the fuse-crate target), or I can scope a **follow-up phase to build the replay test harness** — that's the real lever for those 440 lines, not piecemeal additions here. Want me to capture that harness work as a todo too?
